\displaystyle 2\eta\left(-\frac{C_{H}\alpha\phi^{10/3}}{\kappa^{2}}-36c\phi^{10/3}-C_{D}\lambda_{2}\phi^{2/3}-\frac{1200\lambda_{1}C_{D}}{\kappa^{2}}\phi^{10/3}\right)\mathrm{dist}^{2}_{(j)}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\left(232\eta^{2}\phi^{10/3}-\frac{\eta}{2\beta}\right)\left\|\nabla\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}^{(j)})-\nabla\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}^{*})\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\left(232\eta^{2}\phi^{10/3}+\frac{2\eta}{c}\right)\xi^{2}(r_{1},r_{2},d_{1},d_{2})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\left(20\lambda_{2}^{2}\eta^{2}\phi^{2/3}-\frac{\lambda_{2}\eta}{2}\right)

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{2}\left(\left\|\left[\mathbf{C}_{i}^{(j)}~\mathbf{R}_{i}^{(j)}\right]^{\top}\left[\mathbf{C}_{i}^{(j)}~\mathbf{R}_{i}^{(j)}\right]-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r_{i}}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\left[\mathbf{C}_{i}^{(j)}~\mathbf{P}_{i}^{(j)}\right]^{\top}\left[\mathbf{C}_{i}^{(j)}~\mathbf{P}_{i}^{(j)}\right]-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r_{i}}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\left(120\lambda_{1}^{2}\eta^{2}\phi^{10/3}-\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{1}\eta\right)\left(\left\|\mathbf{A}_{1}^{(j)}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}-\left\|\mathbf{A}_{2}^{(j)}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right)^{2}.

Next, we construct a lower bound of
\left\|\mathbf{A}_{2}^{(j)}\otimes\mathbf{A}_{1}^{(j)}-\mathbf{A}_{2}^{*}\otimes\mathbf{A}_{1}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}
related to
\mathrm{dist}^{2}_{(j)}
. Viewing
\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{A}_{2})
and
\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{A}_{1})
as the factors in
\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{A}_{2})\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{A}_{1})^{\top}
, our first regularizer

\left(\left\|\mathbf{A}_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}-\left\|\mathbf{A}_{2}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right)^{2}=\left(\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{A}_{1})^{\top}\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{A}_{1})-\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{A}_{2})^{\top}\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{A}_{2})\right)^{2}

(84)

can be regarded as a special case of
Wang et al., (2017)
, as well as a generalization of
Tu et al., (2016)
. Define the distance of two vectors up to a sign switch as

\mathrm{dist}^{2}(\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{A}_{i}^{(j)}),\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{A}_{i}^{*}))=\min_{s=\pm 1}\left\|\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{A}_{i}^{(j)})-\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{A}_{i}^{*})s\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2},\quad i=1,2.

For more details of this type of distance, see
Cai and Zhang, (2018)
.
By Lemma
C.3
, since
C_{D}
is small,

\left\|\mathbf{A}_{1}^{(j)}-\mathbf{A}_{1}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\mathbf{A}_{2}^{(j)}-\mathbf{A}_{2}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq 30\phi^{4/3}D\leq 30C_{D}\phi^{2}\leq\phi^{2},\quad i=1,2.

Then, we have
\left\|\mathbf{A}_{i}^{(j)}-\mathbf{A}_{i}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\left\|\mathbf{A}_{i}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}
. Hence, we know that the angle between
\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{A}_{i}^{(j)})
and
\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{A}_{i}^{*})
is an acute angle. Thus,

\mathrm{dist}^{2}(\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{A}_{i}^{(j)}),\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{A}_{i}^{*}))=\left\|\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{A}_{i}^{(j)})-\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{A}_{i}^{*})\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2},\quad i=1,2.

Meanwhile, applying the permutation operator
\mathcal{P}
, the Frobenius norm remains unchanged

\left\|\mathbf{A}_{2}^{(j)}\otimes\mathbf{A}_{1}^{(j)}-\mathbf{A}_{2}^{*}\otimes\mathbf{A}_{1}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}=\left\|\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{A}_{2}^{(j)})\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{A}_{1}^{(j)})^{\top}-\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{A}_{2}^{*})\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{A}_{1}^{*})^{\top}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}.

Recalling the notations defined in Step 3.5 and applying Lemma
C.4
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{A}_{1}^{(j)}-\mathbf{A}_{1}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\mathbf{A}_{2}^{(j)}-\mathbf{A}_{2}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

(85)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{dist}^{2}(\mathbf{Z},\mathbf{Z}^{*})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4(\sqrt{2}-1)\phi^{2}}\left\|\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{Z}^{\top}-\mathbf{Z}^{*}\mathbf{Z}^{*\top}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4(\sqrt{2}-1)\phi^{2}}\left(2\left\|\mathbf{A}_{2}^{(j)}\otimes\mathbf{A}_{1}^{(j)}-\mathbf{A}_{2}^{*}\otimes\mathbf{A}_{1}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right.

\displaystyle+\left.\left\|\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{A}_{1}^{(j)})\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{A}_{1}^{(j)})^{\top}-\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{A}_{1}^{*})\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{A}_{1}^{*})^{\top}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right.

\displaystyle+\left.\left\|\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{A}_{2}^{(j)})\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{A}_{2}^{(j)})^{\top}-\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{A}_{2}^{*})\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{A}_{2}^{*})^{\top}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right).

To erase the last two terms, we note that

\displaystyle\left(\left\|\mathbf{A}_{1}^{(j)}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}-\left\|\mathbf{A}_{2}^{(j)}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right)^{2}

(86)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{Z}^{\top},\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}^{\top}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{Z}^{\top}-\mathbf{Z}^{*}\mathbf{Z}^{*^{\top}},\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}^{\top}-\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}^{*}\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}^{*\top}\right\rangle+\left\langle\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{Z}^{\top},\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}^{*}\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}^{*\top}\right\rangle+\left\langle\mathbf{Z}^{*}\mathbf{Z}^{*^{\top}},\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}^{\top}\right\rangle-\left\langle\mathbf{Z}^{*}\mathbf{Z}^{*^{\top}},\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}^{*}\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}^{*\top}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{Z}^{\top}-\mathbf{Z}^{*}\mathbf{Z}^{*^{\top}},\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}^{\top}-\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}^{*}\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}^{*\top}\right\rangle+2\left\|\mathbf{Z}^{\top}\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}-\underbrace{\left\|\mathbf{Z}^{*\top}\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}_{=0}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{Z}^{\top}-\mathbf{Z}^{*}\mathbf{Z}^{*^{\top}},\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}^{\top}-\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}^{*}\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}}^{*\top}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\|\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{A}_{1}^{(j)})\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{A}_{1}^{(j)})^{\top}-\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{A}_{1}^{*})\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{A}_{1}^{*})^{\top}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{A}_{2}^{(j)})\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{A}_{2}^{(j)})^{\top}-\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{A}_{2}^{*})\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{A}_{2}^{*})^{\top}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle 2\left\|\mathbf{A}_{2}^{(j)}\otimes\mathbf{A}_{1}^{(j)}-\mathbf{A}_{2}^{*}\otimes\mathbf{A}_{1}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}.

Then, combine (
85
) and (
86
), we have

\left\|\mathbf{A}_{1}^{(j)}-\mathbf{A}_{1}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\mathbf{A}_{2}^{(j)}-\mathbf{A}_{2}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq\frac{4}{\phi^{2}}\left\|\mathbf{A}_{2}^{(j)}\otimes\mathbf{A}_{1}^{(j)}-\mathbf{A}_{2}^{*}\otimes\mathbf{A}_{1}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\frac{1}{\phi^{2}}\left(\left\|\mathbf{A}_{1}^{(j)}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}-\left\|\mathbf{A}_{2}^{(j)}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right)^{2}.

(87)

Next, by Lemma
C.3
and
c_{b}\leq 0.01
, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{dist}^{2}_{(j)}

\displaystyle\leq 100\phi^{-4/3}\kappa^{2}\left(\left\|\mathbf{A}_{1}^{(j)}-\mathbf{A}_{1}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\mathbf{A}_{2}^{(j)}-\mathbf{A}_{2}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+24\phi^{-2/3}\sum_{i=1}^{2}\left(\left\|\left[\mathbf{C}_{i}^{(j)}~\mathbf{R}_{i}^{(j)}\right]^{\top}\left[\mathbf{C}_{i}^{(j)}~\mathbf{R}_{i}^{(j)}\right]-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r_{i}}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\left[\mathbf{C}_{i}^{(j)}~\mathbf{P}_{i}^{(j)}\right]^{\top}\left[\mathbf{C}_{i}^{(j)}~\mathbf{P}_{i}^{(j)}\right]-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r_{i}}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right).

Then, we obtain a lower bound:

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{A}_{2}^{(j)}\otimes\mathbf{A}_{1}^{(j)}-\mathbf{A}_{2}^{*}\otimes\mathbf{A}_{1}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

(88)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{\phi^{10/3}}{400\kappa^{2}}\mathrm{dist}^{2}_{(j)}-\frac{1}{4}\left(\left\|\mathbf{A}_{1}^{(j)}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}-\left\|\mathbf{A}_{2}^{(j)}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{3\phi^{8/3}}{50\kappa^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{2}\left(\left\|\left[\mathbf{C}_{i}^{(j)}~\mathbf{R}_{i}^{(j)}\right]^{\top}\left[\mathbf{C}_{i}^{(j)}~\mathbf{R}_{i}^{(j)}\right]-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r_{i}}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\left[\mathbf{C}_{i}^{(j)}~\mathbf{P}_{i}^{(j)}\right]^{\top}\left[\mathbf{C}_{i}^{(j)}~\mathbf{P}_{i}^{(j)}\right]-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r_{i}}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right).

Finally, plugging the lower bound (
88
) into (
83
), we have the following upper bound:

\displaystyle\mathrm{dist}^{2}_{(j+1)}\leq

\displaystyle\left(1-2\eta\left(\frac{\alpha\phi^{10/3}}{800\kappa^{2}}-\frac{C_{h}^{2}C_{D}\alpha\phi^{10/3}}{\kappa^{2}}-36c\phi^{10/3}-C_{D}\lambda_{2}\phi^{2/3}-\frac{1200\lambda_{1}C_{D}}{\kappa^{2}}\phi^{10/3}\right)\right)\mathrm{dist}^{2}_{(j)}

(89)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\eta\left(\eta 232\phi^{10/3}+\frac{2}{c}\right)\xi^{2}(r_{1},r_{2},d_{1},d_{2})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\eta\left(232\eta\phi^{10/3}-\frac{1}{2\beta}\right)\left\|\nabla\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}^{(j)})-\nabla\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}^{*})\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\eta\left(40\lambda_{2}^{2}\eta\phi^{2/3}+\frac{3\alpha\phi^{8/3}}{25\kappa^{2}}-\lambda_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\times\sum_{i=1}^{2}\left(\left\|\left[\mathbf{C}_{i}^{(j)}~\mathbf{R}_{i}^{(j)}\right]^{\top}\left[\mathbf{C}_{i}^{(j)}~\mathbf{R}_{i}^{(j)}\right]-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r_{i}}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\left[\mathbf{C}_{i}^{(j)}~\mathbf{P}_{i}^{(j)}\right]^{\top}\left[\mathbf{C}_{i}^{(j)}~\mathbf{P}_{i}^{(j)}\right]-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r_{i}}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\eta\left(\alpha+480\lambda_{1}^{2}\eta\phi^{10/3}-2\lambda_{1}\right)\left(\left\|\mathbf{A}_{1}^{(j)}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}-\left\|\mathbf{A}_{2}^{(j)}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right)^{2}.

Step 4.
(
Convergence analysis of
\mathrm{dist}^{2}_{(j+1)}
)

For the tuning parameters
\eta,\lambda_{2}
,
\lambda_{1}
and
c
, let

\eta=\frac{\eta_{0}}{\beta\phi^{10/3}},\quad\lambda_{2}=\frac{\alpha\phi^{8/3}}{\kappa^{2}},\quad\lambda_{1}=\alpha,\quad\text{and}\quad c=\frac{C_{D}\alpha}{\kappa^{2}}.

We see that with
\eta_{0}\leq 1/480
and
C_{D}
being small enough, the error is reduced by iteration.

For the coefficient of the third term of (
89
),

232\eta\phi^{10/3}-\frac{1}{2\beta}\leq\frac{232}{480\beta}-\frac{1}{2\beta}\leq 0.

For the fourth term of (
89
),

\displaystyle 40\lambda_{2}^{2}\eta\phi^{2/3}+\frac{3\alpha\phi^{8/3}}{25\kappa^{2}}-\lambda_{2}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{40\alpha^{2}\phi^{8/3}}{480\kappa^{4}\beta}+\frac{3\alpha\phi^{8/3}}{25\kappa^{2}}-\frac{\alpha\phi^{8/3}}{\kappa^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\alpha\phi^{8/3}}{\kappa^{2}}\left(\frac{1}{12}+\frac{3}{25}-1\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 0.

For the fifth term of (
89
),

\displaystyle\alpha+480\lambda_{1}^{2}\eta\phi^{10/3}-2\lambda_{1}\leq

\displaystyle\alpha+\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\beta}-2\alpha\leq(1+1-2)\alpha\leq 0.

Form now on, for the sake of simplicity,
C
will denote a constant whose exact value may change in different contexts. For the second term of (
89
),

\eta\left(\eta 232\phi^{10/3}+\frac{2}{c}\right)\leq\frac{\eta_{0}}{\beta\phi^{10/3}}\left(\frac{232}{480\beta}+\frac{2\kappa^{2}}{C_{D}\alpha}\right)\leq\frac{C\eta_{0}\kappa^{2}}{\alpha\beta\phi^{10/3}}.

For the first term, i.e., the coefficient of
\mathrm{dist}^{2}_{(j)}
,

\displaystyle\frac{\alpha\phi^{10/3}}{800\kappa^{2}}-\frac{C_{h}^{2}C_{D}\alpha\phi^{10/3}}{\kappa^{2}}-36c\phi^{10/3}-C_{D}\lambda_{2}\phi^{2/3}-\frac{1200\lambda_{1}C_{D}}{\kappa^{2}}\phi^{10/3}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\alpha\phi^{10/3}}{\kappa^{2}}\left(\frac{1}{800}-\left(C_{h}^{2}+1237\right)C_{D}\right)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\frac{\alpha\phi^{10/3}}{\kappa^{2}}\times\frac{C_{0}}{2}.

Therefore, we can derive the following recursive relationship:

\mathrm{dist}^{2}_{(j+1)}\leq(1-C_{0}\eta_{0}\alpha\beta^{-1}\kappa^{-2})\mathrm{dist}^{2}_{(j)}+C\eta_{0}\kappa^{2}\alpha^{-1}\beta^{-1}\phi^{-10/3}\xi^{2}(r_{1},r_{2},d_{1},d_{2}).

(90)

When
C_{D}
is small enough,
C_{0}
is a positive constant and
1-C_{0}\eta_{0}\alpha\beta^{-1}\kappa^{-2}
is a constant smaller than 1. Hence, the computational error can be reduced through the proposed gradient decent algorithm.

Step 5.
(
Verification of the conditions
)

In this step, we verify that the conditions (
43
) and (
41
) in the convergence analysis hold recursively.

For
j=0
, since initialization condition
\mathrm{dist}^{2}_{(0)}\leq C_{D}\alpha\beta^{-1}\kappa^{-2}\phi^{2/3}
holds, we have

\displaystyle\left\|[\mathbf{C}_{i}^{(0)}~\mathbf{R}_{i}^{(0)}]\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|[\mathbf{C}_{i}^{(0)}~\mathbf{R}_{i}^{(0)}]-[\mathbf{C}_{i}^{*}~\mathbf{R}_{i}^{*}]\mathbf{O}_{i,u}^{(0)}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\left\|[\mathbf{C}_{i}^{*}~\mathbf{R}_{i}^{*}]\mathbf{O}_{i,u}^{(0)}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

(91)

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{dist}_{(0)}+\phi^{1/3}

\displaystyle\leq(1+c_{b})b^{1/3},

\displaystyle\left\|[\mathbf{C}_{i}^{(0)}~\mathbf{P}_{i}^{(0)}]\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|[\mathbf{C}_{i}^{(0)}~\mathbf{P}_{i}^{(0)}]-[\mathbf{C}_{i}^{*}~\mathbf{P}_{i}^{*}]\mathbf{O}_{i,v}^{(0)}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\left\|[\mathbf{C}_{i}^{*}~\mathbf{P}_{i}^{*}]\mathbf{O}_{i,v}^{(0)}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{dist}_{(0)}+\phi^{1/3}

\displaystyle\leq(1+c_{b})b^{1/3},

and

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{D}_{i}^{(0)}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\mathbf{D}_{i}^{(0)}-\mathbf{O}_{i,u}^{(0)\top}\mathbf{D}_{i}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{i,v}^{(0)}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\left\|\mathbf{O}_{i,u}^{(0)\top}\mathbf{D}_{i}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{i,v}^{(0)}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

(92)

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\mathrm{dist}^{2}_{(0)}}+\phi^{1/3}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(1+c_{b})\phi}{b^{2}},\quad\mathrm{for~}i=1,2.

Then, suppose (
41
) and (
43
) hold at step
j
, for
j+1
, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{dist}^{2}_{(j+1)}

\displaystyle\leq(1-C_{0}\eta_{0}\alpha\beta^{-1}\kappa^{-2})\mathrm{dist}^{2}_{(j)}+C\eta_{0}\kappa^{2}\alpha^{-1}\beta^{-1}\phi^{-10/3}\xi^{2}(r_{1},r_{2},d_{1},d_{2})

(93)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{D}\alpha\phi^{2/3}}{\beta\kappa^{2}}-\eta_{0}\phi^{2/3}\alpha^{2}\beta^{-1}\kappa^{2}\left(\frac{C_{0}C_{D}}{\beta\kappa^{6}}-\frac{C\xi^{2}(r_{1},r_{2},d_{1},d_{2})}{\alpha^{3}\phi^{4}}\right).

Since
\phi^{4}\geq C\beta\kappa^{6}\xi^{2}\alpha^{-3}
for some universally big constant, we can verify that

\frac{C_{2}}{\beta\kappa^{6}}-\frac{C_{1}\xi^{2}(r_{1},r_{2},d_{1},d_{2})}{\alpha^{3}\phi^{4}}\geq 0,

then

\mathrm{dist}^{2}_{(j+1)}\leq\frac{C_{D}\alpha\phi^{2/3}}{\beta\kappa^{2}}.

By the same argument as
j=0
, we can verify that condition (
43
) holds. Then the induction is completed.

Step 6.
(
Construction of the upper bounds
)

In the preceding steps, we proved that (
41
), (
43
), and the recursive relationship (
90
) hold for any
j\geq 0
. By iterating this relationship and summing the resulting geometric series, noting that
\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}(1-C_{0}\eta_{0}\alpha\beta^{-1}\kappa^{-2})^{j}=C_{0}^{-1}\eta_{0}^{-1}\alpha^{-1}\beta\kappa^{2}
, we obtain the upper bound of
\mathrm{dist}^{2}_{(j)}
:

\mathrm{dist}^{2}_{(j)}\leq(1-C_{0}\eta_{0}\alpha\beta^{-1}\kappa^{-2})^{j}\mathrm{dist}^{2}_{(0)}+C\kappa^{4}\alpha^{-2}\phi^{-10/3}\xi^{2}(r_{1},r_{2},d_{1},d_{2}),

(94)

For the error bound of
\mathbf{A}_{1}
and
\mathbf{A}_{2}
, by Lemma
C.3
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{A}_{1}^{(j)}-\mathbf{A}_{1}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\mathbf{A}_{2}^{(j)}-\mathbf{A}_{2}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\phi^{4/3}\mathrm{dist}^{2}_{(j)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\phi^{4/3}(1-C_{0}\eta_{0}\alpha\beta^{-1}\kappa^{-2})^{j}\mathrm{dist}^{2}_{(0)}+C\kappa^{4}\alpha^{-2}\phi^{-2}\xi^{2}(r_{1},r_{2},d_{1},d_{2})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\kappa^{2}(1-C_{0}\eta_{0}\alpha\beta^{-1}\kappa^{-2})^{j}\left(\left\|\mathbf{A}_{1}^{(0)}-\mathbf{A}_{1}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\mathbf{A}_{2}^{(0)}-\mathbf{A}_{2}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+C\kappa^{4}\alpha^{-2}\phi^{-2}\xi^{2}(r_{1},r_{2},d_{1},d_{2}).

Also, for the error bound of
\left\|\mathbf{A}_{2}^{(j)}\otimes\mathbf{A}_{1}^{(j)}-\mathbf{A}_{2}^{*}\otimes\mathbf{A}_{1}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{A}_{2}^{(j)}\otimes\mathbf{A}_{1}^{(j)}-\mathbf{A}_{2}^{*}\otimes\mathbf{A}_{1}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\left\|\left(\mathbf{A}_{2}^{(j)}-\mathbf{A}_{2}^{*}\right)\otimes\mathbf{A}_{1}^{(j)}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+2\left\|\mathbf{A}_{2}^{*}\otimes\left(\mathbf{A}_{1}^{(j)}-\mathbf{A}_{1}^{*}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 8\phi^{2}\left(\left\|\mathbf{A}_{1}^{(j)}-\mathbf{A}_{1}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\mathbf{A}_{2}^{(j)}-\mathbf{A}_{2}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\phi^{2}\kappa^{2}(1-C_{0}\eta_{0}\alpha\beta^{-1}\kappa^{-2})^{j}\left(\left\|\mathbf{A}_{1}^{(0)}-\mathbf{A}_{1}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\mathbf{A}_{2}^{(0)}-\mathbf{A}_{2}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+C\kappa^{4}\alpha^{-2}\xi^{2}(r_{1},r_{2},d_{1},d_{2})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\kappa^{2}(1-C_{0}\eta_{0}\alpha\beta^{-1}\kappa^{-2})^{j}\left(\left\|\mathbf{A}_{2}^{(0)}\otimes\mathbf{A}_{1}^{(0)}-\mathbf{A}_{2}^{*}\otimes\mathbf{A}_{1}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+C\kappa^{4}\alpha^{-2}\xi^{2}(r_{1},r_{2},d_{1},d_{2}).

The last inequality is from (
87
) with the fact that
\left\|\mathbf{A}_{1}^{(0)}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}=\left\|\mathbf{A}_{2}^{(0)}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}
.

C.2
Auxiliary Lemmas

The first lemma states that when the Frobenius norm of estimated parameter matrices are close to their true values, the Frobenius norm of high-order perturbation terms can be controlled by the running error in (
37
).

Lemma C.1
.

Define the following matrices

\displaystyle\mathbf{A}_{1}^{*}=[\mathbf{C}_{1}^{*}~\mathbf{R}_{1}^{*}]\mathbf{D}_{1}^{*}[\mathbf{C}_{1}^{*}~\mathbf{P}_{1}^{*}]^{\top},\mathbf{A}_{2}^{*}=[\mathbf{C}_{2}^{*}~\mathbf{R}_{2}^{*}]\mathbf{D}_{2}^{*}[\mathbf{C}_{2}^{*}~\mathbf{P}_{2}^{*}]^{\top},

\displaystyle\mathbf{A}_{1}=[\mathbf{C}_{1}~\mathbf{R}_{1}]\mathbf{D}_{1}[\mathbf{C}_{1}~\mathbf{P}_{1}]^{\top},\text{ and }\mathbf{A}_{2}=[\mathbf{C}_{2}~\mathbf{R}_{2}]\mathbf{D}_{2}[\mathbf{C}_{2}~\mathbf{P}_{2}]^{\top},

where
\mathbf{D}_{i}^{*}
,
\mathbf{D}_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{r_{i}\times r_{i}}
,
\mathbf{C}_{i}^{*},\mathbf{C}_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{i}\times d_{i}}
, and
\mathbf{R}_{i},\mathbf{R}_{i}^{*},\mathbf{P}_{i},\mathbf{P}_{i}^{*}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{i}\times(r_{i}-d_{i})}
, for
i=1,2
. Meanwhile,
\left\|\mathbf{A}_{1}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}=\left\|\mathbf{A}_{2}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}=\phi
and
\left[\mathbf{C}_{i}^{*}~\mathbf{R}_{i}^{*}\right]^{\top}\left[\mathbf{C}_{i}^{*}~\mathbf{R}_{i}^{*}\right]=\left[\mathbf{C}_{i}^{*}~\mathbf{P}_{i}^{*}\right]^{\top}\left[\mathbf{C}_{i}^{*}~\mathbf{P}_{i}^{*}\right]=b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r_{i}}
for some balance scalar
b
. Suppose that there exists a constant
c
, such that

\left\|\left[\mathbf{C}_{i}~\mathbf{R}_{i}\right]\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq(1+c)b,\quad\left\|\left[\mathbf{C}_{i}~\mathbf{P}_{i}\right]\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq(1+c)b,\text{and }\left\|\mathbf{D}_{i}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\frac{(1+c)\phi}{b^{2}},\quad\text{for }i=1,2.

(95)

For
i=1,2
, let

\begin{gathered}\mathcal{E}_{i,u}=[\mathbf{C}_{i}^{*}~\mathbf{R}_{i}^{*}]\mathbf{O}_{i,u}-[\mathbf{C}_{i}~\mathbf{R}_{i}],\\
\mathcal{E}_{i,v}=[\mathbf{C}_{i}^{*}~\mathbf{P}_{i}^{*}]\mathbf{O}_{i,v}-[\mathbf{C}_{i}~\mathbf{P}_{i}],\\
\mathcal{E}_{i,D}=\mathbf{O}_{i,u}^{\top}\mathbf{D}_{i}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{i,v}-\mathbf{D}_{i},\end{gathered}

where
\mathbf{O}_{i,c}\in\mathbb{O}^{d_{i}\times d_{i}},\mathbf{O}_{i,r},\mathbf{O}_{i,p}\in\mathbb{O}^{(r_{i}-d_{i})\times(r_{i}-d_{i})},\mathbf{O}_{i,u}=

\operatorname{diag}\left(\mathbf{O}_{i,c},\mathbf{O}_{i,r}\right),\mathbf{O}_{i,v}=\operatorname{diag}\left(\mathbf{O}_{i,c},\mathbf{O}_{i,p}\right).

Then, let

\displaystyle\mathbf{H}_{1}:=

\displaystyle-\mathbf{A}_{2}\otimes\left([\mathbf{C}_{1}~\mathbf{R}_{1}]\mathbf{D}_{1}\mathcal{E}_{1,v}^{\top}+[\mathbf{C}_{1}~\mathbf{R}_{1}]\mathcal{E}_{1,D}[\mathbf{C}_{1}~\mathbf{P}_{1}]^{\top}+\mathcal{E}_{1,u}\mathbf{D}_{1}[\mathbf{C}_{1}~\mathbf{P}_{1}]^{\top}\right)

\displaystyle-\left([\mathbf{C}_{2}~\mathbf{R}_{2}]\mathbf{D}_{2}\mathcal{E}_{2,v}^{\top}+[\mathbf{C}_{2}~\mathbf{R}_{2}]\mathcal{E}_{2,D}[\mathbf{C}_{2}~\mathbf{P}_{2}]^{\top}+\mathcal{E}_{2,u}\mathbf{D}_{2}[\mathbf{C}_{2}~\mathbf{P}_{2}]^{\top}\right)\otimes\mathbf{A}_{1},

which contains the first-order perturbation terms, and

\mathbf{H}:=\mathbf{A}_{2}^{*}\otimes\mathbf{A}_{1}^{*}-\mathbf{A}_{2}\otimes\mathbf{A}_{1}+\mathbf{H}_{1},

which represents second- and higher-order terms perturbated from
\mathbf{A}_{2}\otimes\mathbf{A}_{1}
.

Meanwhile, define the distance as

\displaystyle D:=

\displaystyle\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{O}_{i,c}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{O}^{d_{i}\times d}\\
\mathbf{O}_{i,r}^{\prime},\mathbf{O}_{i,p}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{O}^{(r_{i}-d_{i})\times(r_{i}-d_{i})}\\
i=1,2\end{subarray}}\sum_{i=1,2}\Big\{\|\mathbf{C}_{i}-\mathbf{C}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{i,c}^{\prime}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\|\mathbf{R}_{i}-\mathbf{R}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{i,r}^{\prime}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\|\mathbf{P}_{i}-\mathbf{P}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{i,p}^{\prime}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\|\mathbf{D}_{i}-\mathrm{diag}(\mathbf{O}_{i,c}^{\prime},\mathbf{O}_{i,r}^{\prime})^{\top}\mathbf{D}_{i}^{*}\mathrm{diag}(\mathbf{O}_{i,c}^{\prime},\mathbf{O}_{i,p}^{\prime})\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\Big\}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1,2}\Big\{\|\mathbf{C}_{i}-\mathbf{C}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{i,c}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\|\mathbf{R}_{i}-\mathbf{R}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{i,r}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\|\mathbf{P}_{i}-\mathbf{P}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{i,p}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\|\mathbf{D}_{i}-\mathbf{O}_{i,u}^{\top}\mathbf{D}_{i}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{i,v}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\Big\}.

Assume that
D\leq C_{D}\phi^{2/3}
for some constant
C_{D}
. If
b\asymp\phi^{1/3}
, there exists a constant
C_{h}
, such that

\left\|\mathbf{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq C_{h}\phi^{4/3}D.

Proof of Lemma
C.1
.

We start from the decomposing the perturbated matrix
\mathbf{A}_{2}^{*}\otimes\mathbf{A}_{1}^{*}
. Using notations defined above, we split the perturbated matrix to 64 terms and classify them into three types: zeroth-order perturbation, fisrt-order perturbation, and high-order perturbation. Then we control the Frobenius norm of high-order perturbation,
\left\|\mathbf{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}
.

\displaystyle\mathbf{A}_{2}^{*}\otimes\mathbf{A}_{1}^{*}

(96)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[\mathbf{C}_{2}^{*}~\mathbf{R}_{2}^{*}]\mathbf{D}_{2}^{*}[\mathbf{C}_{2}^{*}~\mathbf{P}_{2}^{*}]^{\top}\otimes[\mathbf{C}_{1}^{*}~\mathbf{R}_{1}^{*}]\mathbf{D}_{1}^{*}[\mathbf{C}_{1}^{*}~\mathbf{P}_{1}^{*}]^{\top}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left([\mathbf{C}_{2}~\mathbf{R}_{2}]+\mathcal{E}_{2,u}\right)\left(\mathbf{D}_{2}+\mathcal{E}_{2,D}\right)\left([\mathbf{C}_{2}~\mathbf{P}_{2}]+\mathcal{E}_{2,v}\right)^{\top}\otimes\left([\mathbf{C}_{1}~\mathbf{R}_{1}]+\mathcal{E}_{1,u}\right)\left(\mathbf{D}_{1}+\mathcal{E}_{1,D}\right)\left([\mathbf{C}_{1}~\mathbf{P}_{1}]+\mathcal{E}_{1,v}\right)^{\top}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}[\mathbf{C}_{2}~\mathbf{R}_{2}]\mathbf{D}_{2}[\mathbf{C}_{2}~\mathbf{P}_{2}]^{\top}+[\mathbf{C}_{2}~\mathbf{R}_{2}]\mathbf{D}_{2}\mathcal{E}_{2,v}^{\top}+[\mathbf{C}_{2}~\mathbf{R}_{2}]\mathcal{E}_{2,D}[\mathbf{C}_{2}~\mathbf{P}_{2}]+[\mathbf{C}_{2}~\mathbf{R}_{2}]\mathcal{E}_{2,D}\mathcal{E}_{2,v}^{\top}\\
+\mathcal{E}_{2,u}\mathbf{D}_{2}[\mathbf{C}_{2}~\mathbf{P}_{2}]^{\top}+\mathcal{E}_{2,u}\mathbf{D}_{2}\mathcal{E}_{2,v}^{\top}+\mathcal{E}_{2,u}\mathcal{E}_{2,D}[\mathbf{C}_{2}~\mathbf{P}_{2}]^{\top}+\mathcal{E}_{2,u}\mathcal{E}_{2,D}\mathcal{E}_{2,v}^{\top}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle\otimes\begin{pmatrix}[\mathbf{C}_{1}~\mathbf{R}_{1}]\mathbf{D}_{1}[\mathbf{C}_{1}~\mathbf{P}_{1}]^{\top}+[\mathbf{C}_{1}~\mathbf{R}_{1}]\mathbf{D}_{1}\mathcal{E}_{1,v}^{\top}+[\mathbf{C}_{1}~\mathbf{R}_{1}]\mathcal{E}_{1,D}[\mathbf{C}_{1}~\mathbf{P}_{1}]+[\mathbf{C}_{1}~\mathbf{R}_{1}]\mathcal{E}_{1,D}\mathcal{E}_{1,v}^{\top}\\
+\mathcal{E}_{1,u}\mathbf{D}_{1}[\mathbf{C}_{1}~\mathbf{P}_{1}]^{\top}+\mathcal{E}_{1,u}\mathbf{D}_{1}\mathcal{E}_{1,v}^{\top}+\mathcal{E}_{1,u}\mathcal{E}_{1,D}[\mathbf{C}_{1}~\mathbf{P}_{1}]^{\top}+\mathcal{E}_{1,u}\mathcal{E}_{1,D}\mathcal{E}_{1,v}^{\top}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{A}_{2}\otimes\mathbf{A}_{1}+\mathbf{A}_{2}\otimes\left([\mathbf{C}_{1}~\mathbf{R}_{1}]\mathbf{D}_{1}\mathcal{E}_{1,v}^{\top}+[\mathbf{C}_{1}~\mathbf{R}_{1}]\mathcal{E}_{1,D}[\mathbf{C}_{1}~\mathbf{P}_{1}]^{\top}+\mathcal{E}_{1,u}\mathbf{D}_{1}[\mathbf{C}_{1}~\mathbf{P}_{1}]^{\top}\right)

\displaystyle+\left([\mathbf{C}_{2}~\mathbf{R}_{2}]\mathbf{D}_{2}\mathcal{E}_{2,v}^{\top}+[\mathbf{C}_{2}~\mathbf{R}_{2}]\mathcal{E}_{2,D}[\mathbf{C}_{2}~\mathbf{P}_{2}]^{\top}+\mathcal{E}_{2,u}\mathbf{D}_{2}[\mathbf{C}_{2}~\mathbf{P}_{2}]^{\top}\right)\otimes\mathbf{A}_{1}

\displaystyle+(\text{57 terms containing 2 or more }\mathcal{E}\text{s})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{A}_{2}\otimes\mathbf{A}_{1}-\mathbf{H}_{1}+(\text{57 terms containing 2 or more }\mathcal{E}\text{s}).

Therefore,
\mathbf{H}
is the summation of 57 terms of higher order perturbation. Next, we upper bound
\left\|\mathbf{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}
by upper bounding every piece of
\mathbf{H}
.

\left\|\mathbf{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{57}\left\|\text{the }i\text{th term of }\mathbf{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}.

It can be easily verified that for every
\mathcal{E}
defined above (we just ignore the subscripts),
\left\|\mathcal{E}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq D
. Writing out everyone of the 57 terms we find that the Frobenius of every term can be upper bounded by one of values in
\{CD^{3}
,
CbD^{5/2}
,
C\phi b^{-2}D^{5/2}
,
Cb^{2}D^{2}
,
C\phi b^{-1}D^{2}
,
C\phi^{2}b^{-4}D^{2}
,
Cb^{2}D^{2}
,
C\phi^{2}b^{-3}D^{3/2}
,
C\phi D^{3/2}
,
Cb^{3}D^{3/2}
,
C\phi bD
,
C\phi^{2}b^{-2}D
,
Cb^{4}D\}
. Using
b=\phi^{1/3}
and
D\leq C_{D}\phi^{2/3}
, it is clear that all those values are less than or equal to
C\phi^{4/3}D
, where
C
is another constant. Finally, adding together the 57 upper bounds gives

\left\|\mathbf{H}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq C_{h}\phi^{4/3}D.

∎

The following lemma is adapted from Lemma A.2 in
(
Wang et al.,, 2023
)
, with the spectral norm replaced by the Frobenius norm. The proof follows analogously and is omitted here. It establishes the approximate equivalence between the estimation error of components and that of the aggregated matrix
\mathbf{B}
.

Lemma C.2
.

(
Wang et al.,, 2023
)
Suppose that
\mathbf{B}^{*}=\left[\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{R}^{*}\right]\mathbf{D}^{*}\left[\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{P}^{*}\right]^{\top}
,
\left[\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{R}^{*}\right]^{\top}\left[\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{R}^{*}\right]=b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r_{1}}
,
\left[\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{P}^{*}\right]^{\top}\left[\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{P}^{*}\right]=

b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r_{2}}
,
\phi=\left\|\mathbf{B}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}
, and
\sigma_{r}=\sigma_{r}\left(\mathbf{B}^{*}\right)
. Let
\mathbf{B}=[\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}]\mathbf{D}[\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}]^{\top}
with
\|[\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}]\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\left(1+c\right)b
,
\|[\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}]\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\left(1+c\right)b
, and
\|\mathbf{D}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\left(1+c\right)\phi/b^{2}
for some constant
c>0
. Define

\displaystyle E:=

\displaystyle\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{O}_{c}\in\mathbb{O}^{d\times d}\\
\mathbf{O}_{r},\mathbf{O}_{p}\in\mathbb{O}^{(r-d)\times(r-d)}\end{subarray}}\left(\left\|\left[\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\right]-\left[\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{R}^{*}\right]\operatorname{diag}\left(\mathbf{O}_{c},\mathbf{O}_{r}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\|\left[\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\right]\right.

\displaystyle\left.-\left[\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{P}^{*}\right]\operatorname{diag}\left(\mathbf{O}_{c},\mathbf{O}_{p}\right)\left\|{}_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\right\|\mathbf{D}-\operatorname{diag}\left(\mathbf{O}_{c},\mathbf{O}_{r}\right)^{\top}\mathbf{D}^{*}\operatorname{diag}\left(\mathbf{O}_{c},\mathbf{O}_{p}\right)\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right).

Then, we have

\displaystyle E\leq\left(4b^{-4}+\frac{8b^{2}}{\sigma_{r}^{2}}C_{b}\right)\left\|\mathbf{B}-\mathbf{B}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle+2b^{-2}C_{b}\left(\left\|[\mathbf{C~R}]^{\top}[\mathbf{C~R}]-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|[\mathbf{C~P}]^{\top}[\mathbf{C~P}]-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right)

and

\left\|\mathbf{B}-\mathbf{B}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq 3b^{4}\left[1+4\phi^{2}b^{-6}\left(1+c\right)^{4}\right]E,

where
C_{b}=1+4\phi^{2}b^{-6}\left(\left(1+c\right)^{4}+\left(1+c\right)^{2}\left(2+c\right)^{2}/2\right)
.

Applying Lemma
C.2
on
\mathbf{A}_{1}
and
\mathbf{A}_{2}
, we have the following equivalence relationship between the combined distance
D
defined in Lemma
C.1
and the squared errors of
\mathbf{A}_{1}
and
\mathbf{A}_{2}
.

Lemma C.3
.

Consider
\mathbf{A}_{1}^{*},\mathbf{A}_{1},\mathbf{A}_{2}^{*},\mathbf{A}_{2}
and
D
defined as in Lemma
C.1
, and (
95
) holds. Define
\underline{\sigma}:=\min(\sigma_{1,r_{1}},\sigma_{2,r_{2}}).
Then, we have

\displaystyle D\leq\left(4b^{-4}+\frac{8b^{2}}{\underline{\sigma}^{2}}C_{b}\right)\left(\left\|\mathbf{A}_{1}-\mathbf{A}_{1}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\mathbf{A}_{2}-\mathbf{A}_{2}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right)

(97)

\displaystyle+2C_{b}b^{-2}\sum_{i=1}^{2}\left(\left\|\left[\mathbf{C}_{i}~\mathbf{R}_{i}\right]^{\top}\left[\mathbf{C}_{i}~\mathbf{R}_{i}\right]-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r_{i}}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\left[\mathbf{C}_{i}~\mathbf{P}_{i}\right]^{\top}\left[\mathbf{C}_{i}~\mathbf{P}_{i}\right]-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r_{i}}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right).

In addition,

\left\|\mathbf{A}_{1}-\mathbf{A}_{1}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\mathbf{A}_{2}-\mathbf{A}_{2}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq 6b^{4}D+24\phi^{2}b^{-2}(1+c)^{4}D.

Proof of Lemma
C.3
.

The conditions specified in (
95
) satisfy the premises of Lemma
C.2
. Applying Lemma
C.2
to the pairs
(\mathbf{A}_{1},\mathbf{A}_{1}^{*})
and
(\mathbf{A}_{2},\mathbf{A}_{2}^{*})
separately yields the desired bounds. Specifically, summing the resulting inequalities for
i=1
and
i=2
establishes the first inequality. The second inequality follows directly by applying the reverse bound from Lemma
C.2
and summing over
i=1,2
. This concludes the proof.
∎

The last lemma is useful in analyzing the upper bound of the distance between our estimates and their true values.

Lemma C.4
.

(Lemma 5.4.,
Tu et al., (2016)
) For any
\mathbf{U},\mathbf{X}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times r}
, where
p\geq r
and
\mathbf{X}
is full-rank, define

\mathrm{dist}(\mathbf{U},\mathbf{X})^{2}=\min_{\mathbf{O}\in\mathbb{O}^{r\times r}}\left\|\mathbf{U}-\mathbf{X}\mathbf{O}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2},

we have

\operatorname{dist}(\mathbf{U},\mathbf{X})^{2}\leq\frac{1}{2(\sqrt{2}-1)\sigma_{r}^{2}(\mathbf{X})}\left\|\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\top}-\mathbf{X}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\right\|_{\textup{F}}^{2}.

Appendix D
Statistical Convergence Analysis

In this appendix, we present the stochastic analysis underlying our statistical theory, including verification of the RSC/RSS condition and bounding of the deviation term
\xi
and initialization errors. The conclusions are derived under assumptions presented in Section
4
of the main article. Notations are inherited from Appendix
C
.

D.1
Proof of Theorem
2

Under Assumptions
1
,
2
, and
3
, by Proposition
2
, we have that with probability at least
1-2\exp\left\{-C(p_{1}r_{1}+p_{2}r_{2}+4r_{1}^{2}r_{2}^{2})\right\}
, the RSC/RSS condition holds with
\alpha=\alpha_{\mathrm{RSC}}
and
\beta=\beta_{\mathrm{RSS}}
, whose proof is relegated to Appendix
D.2
. Meanwhile, by Proposition
4
and Proposition
6
, the conditions in Theorem
1
related to
\xi
and
\mathrm{dist}^{2}_{(0)}
are satisfied with high probability, whose proofs are relegated to Appendices
D.3
and
D.4
, respectively.

Therefore, by the conditions of Theorem
2
, the conditions of Theorem
1
are satisfied, implying that
\forall j\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{A}_{1}^{(j)}-\mathbf{A}_{1}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\mathbf{A}_{2}^{(j)}-\mathbf{A}_{2}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\kappa^{2}(1-C_{0}\eta_{0}\alpha_{\mathrm{RSC}}\beta_{\mathrm{RSS}}^{-1}\kappa^{-2})^{j}\left(\left\|\mathbf{A}_{1}^{(0)}-\mathbf{A}_{1}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\mathbf{A}_{2}^{(0)}-\mathbf{A}_{2}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right)+\kappa^{4}\phi^{-2}\alpha_{\mathrm{RSC}}^{-2}\xi^{2}(r_{1},r_{2},d_{1},d_{2}).

For the initial error, we apply Proposition
5
to see that with probability at least
1-C\exp\left(-C(p_{1}r_{1}+p_{2}r_{2})\right)
,

\left\|\mathbf{A}_{1}^{(0)}-\mathbf{A}_{1}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\mathbf{A}_{2}^{(0)}-\mathbf{A}_{2}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\lesssim\phi^{4/3}\mathrm{dist}^{2}_{(0)}\lesssim\phi^{2}\underline{\sigma}^{-4}g_{\min}^{-4}\alpha_{\mathrm{RSC}}^{-2}\tau^{2}M_{1}^{2}\frac{\mathrm{df}_{\mathrm{RRMAR}}}{T}.

In addition, by Proposition
3
, the statistical error is bounded as

\xi(r_{1},r_{2},d_{1},d_{2})\lesssim\tau M_{1}\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{df}_{\mathrm{MARCF}}}{T}},

with probability at least
1-C\exp\left(-C(p_{1}r_{1}+p_{2}r_{2})\right)
.

To ensure the upper bound of the statistical error term dominates the computational error term after
j
iterations, we need

\kappa^{2}(1-C_{0}\eta_{0}\alpha_{\mathrm{RSC}}\beta_{\mathrm{RSS}}^{-1}\kappa^{-2})^{j}\phi^{2}\underline{\sigma}^{-4}g_{\min}^{-4}\alpha_{\mathrm{RSC}}^{-2}\tau^{2}M_{1}^{2}\frac{\mathrm{df}_{\mathrm{RRMAR}}}{T}\lesssim\kappa^{4}\alpha_{\mathrm{RSC}}^{-2}\phi^{-2}\tau^{2}M_{1}^{2}\frac{\mathrm{df}_{\mathrm{MARCF}}}{T}.

(98)

With
\mathrm{df}_{\mathrm{RRMAR}}\leq 2\mathrm{df}_{\mathrm{MARCF}}
, (
98
) gives that when

J\gtrsim\frac{\log\left(\kappa^{-2}g_{\min}^{4}\right)}{\log(1-C_{0}\eta_{0}\alpha_{\mathrm{RSC}}\beta_{\mathrm{RSS}}^{-1}\kappa^{-2})},

the computational error is absorbed, and then

\left\|\mathbf{A}_{1}^{(J)}-\mathbf{A}_{1}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\mathbf{A}_{2}^{(J)}-\mathbf{A}_{2}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\lesssim\kappa^{4}\phi^{-2}\alpha_{\mathrm{RSC}}^{-2}\tau^{2}M_{1}^{2}\frac{\mathrm{df}_{\mathrm{MARCF}}}{T}.

By similar analysis on
\left\|\mathbf{A}_{2}^{(J)}\otimes\mathbf{A}_{1}^{(J)}-\mathbf{A}_{2}^{*}\otimes\mathbf{A}_{1}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}
, we also have

\left\|\mathbf{A}_{2}^{(J)}\otimes\mathbf{A}_{1}^{(J)}-\mathbf{A}_{2}^{*}\otimes\mathbf{A}_{1}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\lesssim\kappa^{4}\alpha_{\mathrm{RSC}}^{-2}\tau^{2}M_{1}^{2}\frac{\mathrm{df}_{\mathrm{MARCF}}}{T}.

∎

D.2
Verification of RSC/RSS Condition

The RSC and RSS properties are defined with respect to the identifiable parameter matrix
\mathbf{A}=\mathbf{A}_{2}\otimes\mathbf{A}_{1}
. The vectorized MARCF model is
\mathbf{y}_{t}=(\mathbf{A}_{2}\otimes\mathbf{A}_{1})\mathbf{y}_{t-1}+\mathbf{e}_{t}
, where
\mathbf{y}_{t}:=\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{Y}_{t})
and
\mathbf{e}_{t}:=\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{E}_{t})
. Then, the least-squares loss function with respect to
\mathbf{A}
can be written as

\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A})=\frac{1}{2T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left\|\mathbf{y}_{t}-\mathbf{A}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}\right\|_{2}^{2}.

(99)

We verify in the following proposition that the RSC/RSS condition holds for any matrices satisfying the restrictions of the MARCF model structure.

Proposition 2
.

Suppose Assumptions
1
and
2
hold. Let
\mathbf{A}=\mathbf{A}_{2}\otimes\mathbf{A}_{1}
and
\mathbf{A}^{\prime}=\mathbf{A}_{2}^{\prime}\otimes\mathbf{A}_{1}^{\prime}
be any two matrices satisfying the MARCF structural decomposition, where
\mathbf{A}_{i}=[\mathbf{C}_{i}~\mathbf{R}_{i}]\mathbf{D}_{i}[\mathbf{C}_{i}~\mathbf{P}_{i}]^{\top}
and
\mathbf{A}_{i}^{\prime}=[\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\prime}~\mathbf{R}_{i}^{\prime}]\mathbf{D}_{i}^{\prime}[\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\prime}~\mathbf{P}_{i}^{\prime}]^{\top}
with dimensions consistent with the model definition. If the sample size
T\gtrsim(p_{1}r_{1}+p_{2}r_{2}+4r_{1}^{2}r_{2}^{2})M_{2}^{-2}\max\left(\tau,\tau^{2}\right)
, then, with probability at least
1-2\exp\left\{-C(p_{1}r_{1}+p_{2}r_{2}+4r_{1}^{2}r_{2}^{2})\right\}
, for such
\mathbf{A}
and
\mathbf{A}^{\prime}
, the loss function
\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}(\cdot)
satisfies the RSC/RSS condition

\frac{\alpha_{\mathrm{RSC}}}{2}\left\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leqslant\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A})-\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\right)-\left\langle\nabla\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\right),\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\right\rangle\leqslant\frac{\beta_{\mathrm{RSS}}}{2}\left\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

(100)

where

\alpha_{\mathrm{RSC}}=\lambda_{\min}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{e}}\right)/(2\mu_{\max}(\mathcal{A})),\quad\beta_{\mathrm{RSS}}=3\lambda_{\max}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{e}}\right)/(2\mu_{\min}(\mathcal{A})),

(101)

and

M_{2}=\lambda_{\min}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{e}}\right)\mu_{\min}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)/\left(\lambda_{\max}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{e}}\right)\mu_{\max}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)\right).

(102)

Proof of Proposition
2
.

Straightforward algebraic manipulation yields

\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A})-\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\right)-\left\langle\nabla\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\right),\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\right\rangle=\frac{1}{2T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\left\|(\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}^{\prime})\mathbf{y}_{t}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}.

(103)

Hence, it suffices to prove that

\frac{\alpha_{\mathrm{RSC}}}{2}\left\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{2T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\left\|(\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}^{\prime})\mathbf{y}_{t}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq\frac{\beta_{\mathrm{RSS}}}{2}\left\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

with high probability.

Based on the moving average representation of
\operatorname{VAR}(1)
, we can rewrite
\mathbf{y}_{t}
as a
\operatorname{VMA}(\infty)
process,

\mathbf{y}_{t}=\mathbf{e}_{t}+\mathbf{A}\mathbf{e}_{t-1}+\mathbf{A}^{2}\mathbf{e}_{t-2}+\mathbf{A}^{3}\mathbf{e}_{t-3}+\cdots

Let
\mathbf{z}=\left(\mathbf{y}_{T-1}^{\top},\mathbf{y}_{T-2}^{\top},\ldots,\mathbf{y}_{0}^{\top}\right)^{\top},\mathbf{e}=\left(\mathbf{e}_{T-1}^{\top},\mathbf{e}_{T-2}^{\top},\ldots,\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\top},\ldots\right)^{\top}
. Now
\mathbf{z}=\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\mathbf{e}
, where
\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}
is a matrix with
Tp_{1}p_{2}
rows and
\infty
columns, defined as

\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}=\left[\begin{array}[]{ccccc}\mathbf{I}_{p_{1}p_{2}}&\mathbf{A}&\mathbf{A}^{2}&\mathbf{A}^{3}&\ldots\\
\mathbf{O}&\mathbf{I}_{p_{1}p_{2}}&\mathbf{A}&\mathbf{A}^{2}&\ldots\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots\\
\mathbf{O}&\mathbf{O}&\mathbf{O}&\mathbf{I}_{p_{1}p_{2}}&\ldots\end{array}\right]=\left[\begin{array}[]{ccccc}\mathbf{I}_{p_{1}p_{2}}&\mathbf{A}_{2}\otimes\mathbf{A}_{1}&(\mathbf{A}_{2}\otimes\mathbf{A}_{1})^{2}&(\mathbf{A}_{2}\otimes\mathbf{A}_{1})^{3}&\ldots\\
\mathbf{O}&\mathbf{I}_{p_{1}p_{2}}&\mathbf{A}_{2}\otimes\mathbf{A}_{1}&(\mathbf{A}_{2}\otimes\mathbf{A}_{1})^{2}&\ldots\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots\\
\mathbf{O}&\mathbf{O}&\mathbf{O}&\mathbf{I}_{p_{1}p_{2}}&\ldots\end{array}\right].

Let
\boldsymbol{\zeta}=\left(\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{T-1}^{\top},\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{T-2}^{\top},\ldots,\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{0}^{\top},\ldots\right)
. By assumption on noise we know that
\mathbf{e}=\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}_{\mathbf{e}}\boldsymbol{\zeta}
where

\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{\mathbf{e}}^{1/2}=\begin{pmatrix}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{e}}^{1/2}&\mathbf{O}&\mathbf{O}&\cdots\\
\mathbf{O}&\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{e}}^{1/2}&\mathbf{O}&\cdots\\
\mathbf{O}&\mathbf{O}&\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{e}}^{1/2}&\cdots\\
\vdots&\vdots&\vdots&\ddots\end{pmatrix}.

Let
\mathbf{\Delta}=\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}^{\prime}
. It suffices to show that for
\left\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}=1
, there exist
0<\alpha\leq\beta
, such that

\frac{\alpha}{2}\leq\frac{1}{2T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\left\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\mathbf{y}_{t}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq\frac{\beta}{2}.

We define the set of unit-norm differences of low-rank Kronecker products as as:

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}(r_{1},r_{2},p_{1},p_{2}):=\Big\{\mathbf{M}:

\displaystyle\mathbf{M}=\mathbf{A}_{2}\otimes\mathbf{A}_{1}-\mathbf{A}_{2}^{\prime}\otimes\mathbf{A}_{1}^{\prime},

(104)

\displaystyle\mathbf{A}_{i},\mathbf{A}_{i}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{i}\times p_{i}},~\text{rank}(\mathbf{A}_{i}),~\text{rank}(\mathbf{A}_{i}^{\prime})\leq r_{i},~\left\|\mathbf{M}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}=1,~i=1,2\Big\}.

For brevity, we denote this set as
\mathcal{S}
when the dimensions and ranks are clear from the context. By construction,
\mathbf{\Delta}\in\mathcal{S}
. Denoting
R_{T}(\mathbf{\Delta}):=\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\left\|\mathbf{\Delta}\mathbf{y}_{t}\right\|^{2}_{2}
, we have

\displaystyle R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbf{y}_{t}^{\top}\mathbf{\Delta}^{\top}\mathbf{\Delta}\mathbf{y}_{t}

(105)

\displaystyle=\left(\mathbf{y}_{T-1}^{\top},\mathbf{y}_{T-2}^{\top},\ldots,\mathbf{y}_{0}^{\top}\right)\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{\Delta}^{\top}\mathbf{\Delta}&&\\
&\ddots&\\
&&\mathbf{\Delta}^{\top}\mathbf{\Delta}\end{pmatrix}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\mathbf{y}_{T-1}\\
\mathbf{y}_{T-2}\\
\vdots\\
\mathbf{y}_{0}\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{z}^{\top}\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}\otimes\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Delta}\right)\mathbf{z}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{e}^{\top}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}\otimes\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Delta}\right)\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\mathbf{e}

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{\top}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{\mathbf{e}}^{1/2}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}\otimes\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Delta}\right)\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{\mathbf{e}}^{1/2}\boldsymbol{\zeta}

\displaystyle:=\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\boldsymbol{\zeta}.

Note that
R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})\geq\mathbb{E}R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})-\sup\limits_{\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\mathcal{S}}|R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})-\mathbb{E}R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})|
, we will derive a lower bound for
\mathbb{E}R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})
and an upper bound for
\sup\limits_{\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\mathcal{S}}|R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})-\mathbb{E}R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})|
to complete the proof of RSC part.

For
\mathbb{E}R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})
, by (
105
) and properties of Frobenius norm,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{\top}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{\mathbf{e}}^{1/2}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}\otimes\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Delta}\right)\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{\mathbf{e}}^{1/2}\boldsymbol{\zeta}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathrm{tr}\left(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{\mathbf{e}}^{1/2}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}\otimes\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Delta}\right)\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{\mathbf{e}}^{1/2}\right)

\displaystyle=\left\|(\mathbf{I}_{T}\otimes\boldsymbol{\Delta})\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{\mathbf{e}}^{1/2}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq T\lambda_{\min}(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{\top})\lambda_{\min}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{e}}).

For
|R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})-\mathbb{E}R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})|
, noting that

\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq\left\|\mathbf{I}_{T}\otimes\boldsymbol{\Delta}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\left\|\mathbf{I}_{T}\otimes\boldsymbol{\Delta}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{\mathbf{e}}^{1/2}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{4}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{4}\leq T\lambda_{\max}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{e}}\right)\lambda_{\max}\left(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}\right)

(106)

and

\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\right\|_{\text{op }}\leq\left\|\mathbf{I}_{T}\otimes\boldsymbol{\Delta}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{\mathbf{e}}^{1/2}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\leq\lambda_{\max}^{2}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{e}}\right)\lambda_{\max}^{2}\left(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}\right),

by Hanson-Wright inequality (Lemma
D.5
), we have that for any
x\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(|R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})-\mathbb{E}R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})|\geq\tau x\right)

(107)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\exp\left\{-C\min\left(\frac{x}{\lambda_{\max}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{e}}\right)\lambda_{\max}\left(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}\right)},\frac{x^{2}}{T\lambda_{\max}^{2}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{e}}\right)\lambda_{\max}^{2}\left(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}\right)}\right)\right\},

where
C
is a constant.

For any
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
, consider an
\varepsilon
-net of
\mathcal{S}
with respect to the Frobenius norm, denoted by
\overline{\mathcal{S}}
. Then for any
\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\mathcal{S}
, there exists
\overline{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\in\overline{\mathcal{S}}
, such that
\left\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}-\overline{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\varepsilon
. By Lemma
D.6
, we have

|\overline{\mathcal{S}}|\leq\left(\frac{15}{\varepsilon}\right)^{4p_{1}r_{1}+4p_{2}r_{2}+16r_{1}^{2}r_{2}^{2}}.

(108)

Therefore,

\displaystyle|R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})-\mathbb{E}R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{t=0}^{T}\left(\left\|\mathbf{y}_{t}^{\top}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{p_{1}p_{2}}\mathrm{vec}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}-\mathbb{E}\left\|\mathbf{y}_{t}^{\top}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{p_{1}p_{2}}\mathrm{vec}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\right)\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\mathrm{vec}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})^{\top}\sum_{t=0}^{T}\left(\mathbf{y}_{t}\mathbf{y}_{t}^{\top}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{p_{1}p_{2}}-\mathbb{E}\mathbf{y}_{t}\mathbf{y}_{t}^{\top}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{p_{1}p_{2}}\right)\mathrm{vec}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})\right|

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\left|\mathrm{vec}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})^{\top}\mathbf{M}(\mathbf{y}_{t})\mathrm{vec}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left|\mathrm{vec}(\overline{\boldsymbol{\Delta}})^{\top}\mathbf{M}(\mathbf{y}_{t})\mathrm{vec}(\overline{\boldsymbol{\Delta}})\right|+2\left|\mathrm{vec}(\overline{\boldsymbol{\Delta}})^{\top}\mathbf{M}(\mathbf{y}_{t})(\mathrm{vec}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})-\mathrm{vec}(\overline{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}))\right|

\displaystyle+\left|(\mathrm{vec}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})^{\top}-\mathrm{vec}(\overline{\boldsymbol{\Delta}})^{\top})\mathbf{M}(\mathbf{y}_{t})(\mathrm{vec}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})-\mathrm{vec}(\overline{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}))\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\max_{\overline{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\in\overline{\mathcal{S}}}|R_{T}(\overline{\boldsymbol{\Delta}})-\mathbb{E}R_{T}(\overline{\boldsymbol{\Delta}})|+(2\varepsilon+\varepsilon^{2})\sup_{\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\mathcal{S}}|R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})-\mathbb{E}R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})|.

When
\varepsilon<1
, we have

\sup_{\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\mathcal{S}}|R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})-\mathbb{E}R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})|\leq(1-3\varepsilon)^{-1}\max_{\overline{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\in\overline{\mathcal{S}}}|R_{T}(\overline{\boldsymbol{\Delta}})-\mathbb{E}R_{T}(\overline{\boldsymbol{\Delta}})|.

Letting
\varepsilon=0.1
, by union bounds and (
107
),

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\mathcal{S}}|R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})-\mathbb{E}R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})|\geq\tau x\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{\overline{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\in\overline{\mathcal{S}}}|R_{T}(\overline{\boldsymbol{\Delta}})-\mathbb{E}R_{T}(\overline{\boldsymbol{\Delta}})|\geq(1-3\varepsilon)\tau x\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{\overline{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\in\overline{\mathcal{S}}}\mathbb{P}\left(|R_{T}(\overline{\boldsymbol{\Delta}})-\mathbb{E}R_{T}(\overline{\boldsymbol{\Delta}})|\geq\frac{1}{2}\tau x\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\times 150^{4p_{1}r_{1}+4p_{2}r_{2}+16r_{1}^{2}r_{2}^{2}}\exp\left\{-C\min\left(\frac{x}{\lambda_{\max}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{e}}\right)\lambda_{\max}\left(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}\right)},\frac{x^{2}}{T\lambda_{\max}^{2}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{e}}\right)\lambda_{\max}^{2}\left(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}\right)}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\exp\left\{21(p_{1}r_{1}+p_{2}r_{2}+4r_{1}^{2}r_{2}^{2})-C\min\left(\frac{x}{\lambda_{\max}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{e}}\right)\lambda_{\max}\left(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}\right)},\frac{x^{2}}{T\lambda_{\max}^{2}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{e}}\right)\lambda_{\max}^{2}\left(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}\right)}\right)\right\}.

Here we take
x=T\lambda_{\min}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{e}}\right)\lambda_{\min}\left(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}\right)/(2\tau)
and define

M_{2}:=\lambda_{\min}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{e}}\right)\lambda_{\min}\left(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}\right)/\lambda_{\max}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{e}}\right)\lambda_{\max}\left(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}\right)\leq 1,

then

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\mathbb{S}}|R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})-\mathbb{E}R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})|\geq\frac{T}{2}\lambda_{\min}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{e}}\right)\lambda_{\min}\left(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}\right)\right)

(109)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\exp\left\{21(p_{1}r_{1}+p_{2}r_{2}+4r_{1}^{2}r_{2}^{2})-C\min\left(\frac{M_{2}}{2\tau},\frac{M^{2}_{2}}{4\tau^{2}}\right)T\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\exp\left\{21(p_{1}r_{1}+p_{2}r_{2}+4r_{1}^{2}r_{2}^{2})-CM_{2}^{2}\min\left(\tau^{-1},\tau^{-2}\right)T\right\}.

Hence, when
T\gtrsim(p_{1}r_{1}+p_{2}r_{2}+4r_{1}^{2}r_{2}^{2})M_{2}^{-2}\max\left(\tau,\tau^{2}\right)
, we have that with probability at least
1-2\exp\left\{-C(p_{1}r_{1}+p_{2}r_{2}+4r_{1}^{2}r_{2}^{2})\right\}
,

R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})\geq\frac{T}{2}\lambda_{\min}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{e}}\right)\lambda_{\min}\left(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}\right).

Then the RSC coefficient
\alpha_{\mathrm{RSC}}=\lambda_{\min}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{e}}\right)\lambda_{\min}\left(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}\right)/2.

As for RSS part, similarly, we have
R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})\leq\mathbb{E}R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})+\sup\limits_{\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\mathcal{S}}|R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})-\mathbb{E}R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})|
and
\mathbb{E}R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})\leq T\lambda_{\max}(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{\top})\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{e}}).
Since the event

\left\{\sup\limits_{\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\mathcal{S}}|R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})-\mathbb{E}R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})|\leq T\lambda_{\min}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{e}}\right)\lambda_{\min}\left(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}\right)/2\right\}

implies

\left\{\sup\limits_{\boldsymbol{\Delta}\in\mathcal{S}}|R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})-\mathbb{E}R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})|\leq T\lambda_{\max}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{e}}\right)\lambda_{\max}\left(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}\right)/2\right\},

we have when the event above occurs,

R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})\leq\frac{3T}{2}\lambda_{\max}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{e}}\right)\lambda_{\max}\left(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}\right).

(110)

Thus
\beta_{\mathrm{RSS}}:=3\lambda_{\max}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{e}}\right)\lambda_{\max}\left(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}\right)/2.

Finally, since
\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}
is related to
\operatorname{VMA}(\infty)
process, by the spectral measure of ARMA process discussed in
Basu and Michailidis, (2015)
we replace
\lambda_{\max}\left(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}\right)
and
\lambda_{\min}\left(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}\right)
with
1/\mu_{\min}(\mathcal{A})
and
1/\mu_{\max}(\mathcal{A})
, respectively.
∎

D.3
Property of Deviation Bound

In this appendix, we derive an upper bound for
\xi
defined in
2
, which demonstrates the benefit of dimension reduction conferred by the MARCF model.

Proposition 3
.

Under Assumptions
1
and
2
, if
T\gtrsim(p_{1}r_{1}+p_{2}r_{2}+4r_{1}^{2}r_{2}^{2})M_{2}^{-2}\max\left(\tau,\tau^{2}\right)
, then with probability as least
1-2\exp\left(-C(p_{1}r_{1}+p_{2}r_{2})\right)
,

\xi(r_{1},r_{2},d_{1},d_{2})\lesssim\tau M_{1}\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{df}_{\mathrm{MARCF}}}{T}},

where
M_{1}=\lambda_{\max}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{e}}\right)/\mu_{\min}^{1/2}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)=\lambda_{\max}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{e}}\right)\lambda_{\max}^{1/2}\left(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}\right)
,
\mathrm{df}_{\mathrm{MARCF}}=p_{1}(2r_{1}-d_{1})+p_{2}(2r_{2}-d_{2})+r_{1}^{2}+r_{2}^{2}
, and
M_{2}
is defined in Proposition
2
.

Proof of Proposition
3
.

We first define the parameter spaces for
\mathbf{A}_{1}
and
\mathbf{A}_{2}
in the MARCF model. Specifically, we consider the following set of matrices with unit Frobenius norm and common column and row subspaces, denoted by
\mathcal{W}(r,d;p)
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}(r,d;p):=\left\{\mathbf{W}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times p}:\mathbf{W}=[\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}]\mathbf{D}[\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}]^{\top},\mathbf{C}\in\mathbb{O}^{p\times d},\mathbf{R},\mathbf{P}\in\mathbb{O}^{p\times(r-d)},\right.

(111)

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathbf{C},\mathbf{R}\right\rangle=\left\langle\mathbf{C},\mathbf{P}\right\rangle=0,\left.\textrm{ and }\left\|\mathbf{W}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}=1\right\}.

Let
\overline{\mathcal{W}}(r,d;p)
be the
\varepsilon/2
-net of
\mathcal{W}(r,d,p)
with respect to the Frobenius norm. By Lemma
D.8
,
\overline{\mathcal{W}}(r,d;p)\subset\mathcal{W}(r,d;p)
, and

|\overline{\mathcal{W}}(r,d;p)|\leq\left(\frac{48}{\varepsilon}\right)^{p(2r-d)+r^{2}}.

Define

\mathcal{V}(r_{1},r_{2},d_{1},d_{2}):=\left\{\mathbf{W}_{2}\otimes\mathbf{W}_{1}:\mathbf{W}_{2}\in\mathcal{W}(r_{2},d_{2};p_{2}),\mathbf{W}_{1}\in\mathcal{W}(r_{1},d_{1};p_{1})\right\},

then

\overline{\mathcal{V}}(r_{1},r_{2},d_{1},d_{2}):=\left\{\overline{\mathbf{W}}_{2}\otimes\overline{\mathbf{W}}_{1}:\overline{\mathbf{W}}_{2}\in\overline{\mathcal{W}}(r_{2},d_{2};p_{2}),\overline{\mathbf{W}}_{1}\in\overline{\mathcal{W}}(r_{1},d_{1};p_{1})\right\}
is an
\varepsilon
-net of
\mathcal{V}(r_{1},r_{2},d_{1},d_{2})
, which can be directly verified. Meanwhile,

|\overline{\mathcal{V}}(r_{1},r_{2},d_{1},d_{2})|\leq\left(\frac{48}{\varepsilon}\right)^{2(p_{1}r_{1}+p_{2}r_{2})-p_{1}d_{1}-p_{2}d_{2}+r_{1}^{2}+r_{2}^{2}}.

(112)

In addition, note that
\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{*})=(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{e}_{t}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top})/T
, we have

\xi(r_{1},r_{2},d_{1},d_{2})=\sup_{\mathbf{A}\in\mathcal{V}(r_{1},r_{2},d_{1},d_{2})}\left\langle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{e}_{t}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top},\mathbf{A}\right\rangle.

In the following, we start from establishing an upper bound for
\xi
when there is no common space, i.e.,
d_{1}=d_{2}=0
. In this case, elements of
\mathbf{W}_{1}
and
\mathbf{W}_{2}
are merely low rank matrices.

For every
\mathbf{A}=\mathbf{W}_{2}\otimes\mathbf{W}_{1}\in\mathcal{V}(r_{1},r_{2},0,0)
, let
\overline{\mathbf{A}}=\overline{\mathbf{W}}_{2}\otimes\overline{\mathbf{W}}_{1}\in\overline{\mathcal{V}}(r_{1},r_{2},0,0)
be its covering matrix. By splitting SVD with common space (Lemma
D.7
), we know that
\mathbf{W}_{2}-\overline{\mathbf{W}}_{2}
can be decomposed as
\mathbf{W}_{2}-\overline{\mathbf{W}}_{2}=\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2,1}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2,2}
, where
\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2,1},\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2,2}
are both rank-
r_{2}
and
\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2,1},\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2,2}\right\rangle=0
. For
\mathbf{W}_{1}-\overline{\mathbf{W}}_{1}
, there exist
\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{1,1}
and
\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{1,2}
following the same property. Then with Cauchy’s inequality and
\left\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{i,1}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{i,2}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}=\left\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{i,1}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{i,1}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}
we know that
\left\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{i,1}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\left\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{i,2}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\sqrt{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{i,1}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{i,2}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\sqrt{2}\varepsilon
, for
i=1,2
. Since
\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{i,j}/\left\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{i,j}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\in\mathcal{W}(r_{i},0;p_{i})
we have

\displaystyle\left\langle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{e}_{t}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top},\mathbf{A}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\langle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{e}_{t}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top},\overline{\mathbf{A}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle+\left\langle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{e}_{t}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top},(\mathbf{W}_{2}-\overline{\mathbf{W}}_{2})\otimes\mathbf{W}_{1}\right\rangle+\left\langle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{e}_{t}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top},\overline{\mathbf{W}}_{2}\otimes(\mathbf{W}_{1}-\overline{\mathbf{W}}_{1})\right\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\langle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{e}_{t}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top},\overline{\mathbf{A}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle+\left\langle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{e}_{t}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top},\frac{\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2,1}}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2,1}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}}\otimes\mathbf{W}_{1}\right\rangle\left\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2,1}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\left\langle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{e}_{t}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top},\frac{\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2,1}}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2,1}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}}\otimes\mathbf{W}_{1}\right\rangle\left\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2,2}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle+\left\langle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{e}_{t}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top},\overline{\mathbf{W}}_{2}\otimes\frac{\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{1,1}}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{1,1}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}}\right\rangle\left\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{1,1}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\left\langle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{e}_{t}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top},\overline{\mathbf{W}}_{2}\otimes\frac{\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{1,2}}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{1,2}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}}\right\rangle\left\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{1,2}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\max_{\overline{A}\in\overline{\mathcal{V}}(r_{1},r_{2},0,0)}\left\langle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{e}_{t}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top},\overline{\mathbf{A}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle+\xi(r_{1},r_{2},0,0)\left(\left\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2,1}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\left\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2,2}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\left\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{1,1}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\left\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{1,2}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\max_{\overline{A}\in\overline{\mathcal{V}}(r_{1},r_{2},0,0)}\left\langle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{e}_{t}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top},\overline{\mathbf{A}}\right\rangle+2\sqrt{2}\varepsilon\xi(r_{1},r_{2},0,0).

Hence,

\xi(r_{1},r_{2},0,0)\leq(1-2\sqrt{2}\varepsilon)^{-1}\max_{\overline{A}\in\overline{\mathcal{V}}(r_{1},r_{2},0,0)}\left\langle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{e}_{t}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top},\overline{\mathbf{A}}\right\rangle.

Define
R_{T}(\mathbf{M}):=\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\left\|\mathbf{M}\mathbf{y}_{t}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}
and
S_{T}(\mathbf{M}):=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left\langle\mathbf{e}_{t},\mathbf{M}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}\right\rangle
for any
p_{1}p_{2}\times p_{1}p_{2}
real matrix
\mathbf{M}
with unit Frobenius norm. By the proof of LemmaS5 in
Wang et al., (2024)
, for any
z_{1}
and
z_{2}\geq 0
,

\mathbb{P}\left[\left\{S_{T}(\mathbf{M})\geq z_{1}\right\}\cap\left\{R_{T}(\mathbf{M})\leq z_{2}\right\}\right]\leq\exp\left(-\frac{z_{1}^{2}}{2\tau^{2}\lambda_{\max}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{e}}\right)z_{2}}\right).

With the derivation of (
109
) and (
110
), when
T\gtrsim(p_{1}r_{1}+p_{2}r_{2}+4r_{1}^{2}r_{2}^{2})M_{2}^{-2}\max\left(\tau,\tau^{2}\right)
, using proper constants
C
here, we have that

\mathbb{P}\left(R_{T}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})\geq\frac{3T}{2}\lambda_{\max}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{e}}\right)\lambda_{\max}\left(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}\right)\right)\leq 2\exp\{-15(p_{1}r_{1}+p_{2}r_{2}+4r_{1}^{2}r_{2}^{2})\}.

Then, using the pieces above, we have for any
x\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\xi(r_{1},r_{2},0,0)\geq x\right)

(113)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{\overline{A}\in\overline{\mathcal{V}}(r_{1},r_{2},0,0)}\left\langle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{e}_{t}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top},\overline{\mathbf{A}}\right\rangle\geq(1-2\sqrt{2}\varepsilon)x\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{\overline{A}\in\overline{\mathcal{V}}(r_{1},r_{2},0,0)}\mathbb{P}\left(S_{T}(\overline{\mathbf{A}})\geq(1-2\sqrt{2}\varepsilon)Tx\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{\overline{A}\in\overline{\mathcal{V}}(r_{1},r_{2},0,0)}\mathbb{P}\left(\left\{S_{T}(\overline{\mathbf{A}})\geq(1-2\sqrt{2}\varepsilon)Tx\right\}\bigcap\left\{R_{T}(\overline{\mathbf{A}})\leq\frac{3T}{2}\lambda_{\max}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{e}}\right)\lambda_{\max}\left(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}\right)\right\}\right)

\displaystyle+\sum_{\overline{A}\in\overline{\mathcal{V}}(r_{1},r_{2},0,0)}\mathbb{P}\left(R_{T}(\overline{\mathbf{A}})\geq\frac{3T}{2}\lambda_{\max}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{e}}\right)\lambda_{\max}\left(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|\overline{\mathcal{V}}(r_{1},r_{2},0,0)|\left(\exp\left\{-\frac{(1-2\sqrt{2}\varepsilon)^{2}Tx^{2}}{3\tau^{2}\lambda_{\max}^{2}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{e}}\right)\lambda_{\max}\left(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}\right)}\right\}+\exp\left\{-15(p_{1}r_{1}+p_{2}r_{2}+4r_{1}^{2}r_{2}^{2})\right\}\right).

Here we take
\varepsilon=0.1
and
x=4\sqrt{3}\tau\lambda_{\max}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{e}}\right)\lambda_{\max}^{1/2}\left(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}\right)\sqrt{(2p_{1}r_{1}+2p_{2}r_{2}+r_{1}^{2}+r_{2}^{2})/T}
, then

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\xi(r_{1},r_{2},0,0)\geq x\right)

(114)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\frac{48}{0.1}\right)^{2(p_{1}r_{1}+p_{2}r_{2})+r_{1}^{2}+r_{2}^{2}}

\displaystyle\times\left(\exp\left\{-\frac{Tx^{2}}{6\tau^{2}\lambda_{\max}^{2}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{e}}\right)\lambda_{\max}\left(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}\right)}\right\}+\exp\left\{-15(p_{1}r_{1}+p_{2}r_{2}+4r_{1}^{2}r_{2}^{2})\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp\left\{(7-8)(2p_{1}r_{1}+2p_{2}r_{2}+r_{1}^{2}+r_{2}^{2})\right\}

\displaystyle+2\exp\left\{7(2p_{1}r_{1}+2p_{2}r_{2}+r_{1}^{2}+r_{2}^{2})-15(p_{1}r_{1}+p_{2}r_{2}+4r_{1}^{2}r_{2}^{2})\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\exp\{-C(p_{1}r_{1}+p_{2}r_{2})\}.

Define

\mathrm{df}_{\mathrm{RRMAR}}=2p_{1}r_{1}+2p_{2}r_{2}+r_{1}^{2}+r_{2}^{2}
and
M_{1}=\lambda_{\max}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{e}}\right)/\mu_{\min}^{1/2}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)=\lambda_{\max}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{e}}\right)\lambda_{\max}^{1/2}\left(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}\right)
.
Therefore, when
T\gtrsim(p_{1}r_{1}+p_{2}r_{2}+4r_{1}^{2}r_{2}^{2})M_{2}^{-2}\max\left(\tau,\tau^{2}\right)
, we have that with probability at least
1-C\exp\{-C(p_{1}r_{1}+p_{2}r_{2})\}
,

\xi(r_{1},r_{2},0,0)\lesssim\tau M_{1}\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{df}_{\mathrm{RRMAR}}}{T}}.

Next, we construct the upper bound of
\xi
when common spaces exist. By Lemma
D.7
, now
\mathbf{W}_{2}-\overline{\mathbf{W}}_{2}
can be decomposed as
\mathbf{W}_{2}-\overline{\mathbf{W}}_{2}=\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2,1}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2,2}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2,3}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2,4}
, where
\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2,1},\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2,2}
are both rank-
r_{2}
with common dimension
d_{2}
.
\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2,3},\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2,4}
are rank-
r_{2}
. Moreover,
\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2,j},\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2,k}\right\rangle=0
for any
j,k=1,2,3,4,j\neq k
. Then with Cauchy’s inequality and
\left\|\sum_{s=1}^{4}\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{i,s}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}=\sum_{s=1}^{4}\left\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{i,s}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}
we know that
\sum_{s=1}^{4}\left\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{i,s}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq 2\left\|\sum_{s=1}^{4}\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{i,s}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq 2\varepsilon,i=1,2.
Similarly, we can decompose each side of the Kronecker product into four parts. The first two parts contain common space while the last two do not. Thus,
\xi(r_{1},r_{2},d_{1},d_{2})
can be upper bounded by the covering of
\mathcal{V}(r_{1},r_{2},d_{1},d_{2})
and
\xi(r_{1},r_{2},0,0)
as following:

\displaystyle\left\langle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{e}_{t}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top},\mathbf{A}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\langle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{e}_{t}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top},\overline{\mathbf{A}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle+\sum_{s=1}^{4}\left\langle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{e}_{t}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top},\frac{\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2,s}}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2,s}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}}\otimes\mathbf{W}_{1}\right\rangle\left\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2,s}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{s=1}^{4}\left\langle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{e}_{t}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top},\overline{\mathbf{W}}_{2}\otimes\frac{\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{1,s}}{\left\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{1,s}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}}\right\rangle\left\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{1,s}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\max_{\overline{A}\in\overline{\mathcal{V}}(r_{1},r_{2},d_{1},d_{2})}\left\langle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{e}_{t}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top},\overline{\mathbf{A}}\right\rangle+4\varepsilon\xi(r_{1},r_{2},0,0)+4\varepsilon\xi(r_{1},r_{2},d_{1},d_{2})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\max_{\overline{A}\in\overline{\mathcal{V}}(r_{1},r_{2},d_{1},d_{2})}\left\langle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{e}_{t}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top},\overline{\mathbf{A}}\right\rangle+4\varepsilon\xi(r_{1},r_{2},0,0)+4\varepsilon\xi(r_{1},r_{2},d_{1},d_{2}).

Therefore,

\xi(r_{1},r_{2},d_{1},d_{2})\leq(1-4\varepsilon)^{-1}\left(\max_{\overline{A}\in\overline{\mathcal{V}}(r_{1},r_{2},d_{1},d_{2})}\left\langle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{e}_{t}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top},\overline{\mathbf{A}}\right\rangle+4\varepsilon\xi(r_{1},r_{2},0,0)\right).

Similar to the derivation of
\xi(r_{1},r_{2},0,0)
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{\overline{A}\in\overline{\mathcal{V}}(r_{1},r_{2},d_{1},d_{2})}\left\langle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{e}_{t}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top},\overline{\mathbf{A}}\right\rangle\geq x\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|\overline{\mathcal{V}}(r_{1},r_{2},d_{1},d_{2})|\left(\exp\left\{-\frac{Tx^{2}}{3\tau^{2}\lambda_{\max}^{2}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{e}}\right)\lambda_{\max}\left(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}\right)}\right\}+\exp\left\{-C(p_{1}r_{1}+p_{2}r_{2}+4r_{1}^{2}r_{2}^{2})\right\}\right).

Define

\mathrm{df}_{\mathrm{MARCF}}:=p_{1}(2r_{1}-d_{1})+p_{2}(2r_{2}-d_{2})+r_{1}^{2}+r_{2}^{2}.

Taking
\varepsilon=0.1
and
x=C\tau\lambda_{\max}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{e}}\right)\lambda_{\max}^{1/2}\left(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}\right)\sqrt{\mathrm{df}_{\mathrm{MARCF}}/T}
and with (
112
), similarly we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{\overline{A}\in\overline{\mathcal{V}}(r_{1},r_{2},d_{1},d_{2})}\left\langle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{e}_{t}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top},\overline{\mathbf{A}}\right\rangle\gtrsim\tau M_{1}\sqrt{\mathrm{df}_{\mathrm{MARCF}}/T}\right)\leq\exp\left\{-C(p_{1}r_{1}+p_{2}r_{2})\right\}

Combining the upper bound of
\xi(r_{1},r_{2},0,0)
, we have

\xi(r_{1},r_{2},d_{1},d_{2})\lesssim\tau M_{1}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{df}_{\mathrm{MARCF}}}{T}}+\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{df}_{\mathrm{RRMAR}}}{T}}\right),

with probability at least
1-C\exp\left(-C(p_{1}r_{1}+p_{2}r_{2})\right)
. Moreover, since
\mathrm{df}_{\mathrm{RRMAR}}\leq 2\mathrm{df}_{\mathrm{MARCF}}
,

\xi(r_{1},r_{2},d_{1},d_{2})\lesssim\tau M_{1}\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{df}_{\mathrm{MARCF}}}{T}}.

∎

With this upper bound, we verify the condition on the upper bound of
\xi
in Theorem
1
.

Proposition 4
.

Under Assumptions
1
and
2
, if
T\gtrsim(p_{1}r_{1}+p_{2}r_{2}+4r_{1}^{2}r_{2}^{2})M_{2}^{-2}\max\left(\tau,\tau^{2}\right)
as well as
T\gtrsim\kappa^{2}\underline{\sigma}^{-4}\alpha_{\mathrm{RSC}}^{-3}\beta_{\mathrm{RSS}}\tau^{2}M_{1}^{2}(p_{1}r_{1}+p_{2}r_{2})
, then we have that, with probability as least
1-C\exp\left(-C(p_{1}r_{1}+p_{2}r_{2})\right)
,

\xi^{2}\lesssim\frac{\phi^{4}\alpha_{\mathrm{RSC}}^{3}}{\kappa^{6}\beta_{\mathrm{RSS}}}.

Proof of Proposition
4
.

By Proposition
3
, we know that with probability at least
1-C\exp\left(-C(p_{1}r_{1}+p_{2}r_{2})\right)
,

\xi(r_{1},r_{2},d_{1},d_{2})\lesssim\tau M_{1}\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{df}_{\mathrm{MARCF}}}{T}}.

Then, when
T\gtrsim\kappa^{2}\underline{\sigma}^{-4}\alpha_{\mathrm{RSC}}^{-3}\beta_{\mathrm{RSS}}\tau^{2}M_{1}^{2}(p_{1}r_{1}+p_{2}r_{2})\gtrsim\kappa^{2}\underline{\sigma}^{-4}\alpha_{\mathrm{RSC}}^{-3}\beta_{\mathrm{RSS}}\tau^{2}M_{1}^{2}\mathrm{df}_{\mathrm{MARCF}}
, we have

\xi^{2}\lesssim\frac{\tau^{2}M_{1}^{2}\mathrm{df}_{\mathrm{MARCF}}}{\kappa^{2}\underline{\sigma}^{-4}\alpha_{\mathrm{RSC}}^{-3}\beta_{\mathrm{RSS}}\tau^{2}M_{1}^{2}\mathrm{df}_{\mathrm{MARCF}}}=\frac{\phi^{4}\alpha_{\mathrm{RSC}}^{3}}{\kappa^{6}\beta_{\mathrm{RSS}}}.

∎

D.4
Properties of Initialization

Our initialization begins with finding the solution of the reduced-rank least squares problem of RRMAR model introduced in
Xiao et al., (2023)
:

\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}_{1}^{\mathrm{RR}},\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}_{2}^{\mathrm{RR}}:=\argmin_{\mathrm{rank}(\mathbf{A}_{i})\leq r_{i},i=1,2}\frac{1}{2T}\sum_{i=1}^{T}\left\|\mathbf{Y}_{t}-\mathbf{A}_{1}\mathbf{Y}_{t-1}\mathbf{A}_{2}^{\top}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}.

(115)

Then we let
\widehat{\mathbf{A}}_{1}^{\mathrm{RR}}
and
\widehat{\mathbf{A}}_{2}^{\mathrm{RR}}
to be the rescaled estimation with equal Frobenius norm. Multiple numerical approaches can be applied to find the optimal solution. For example, the alternating least squares method (RR.LS) and alternating canonical correlation analysis method (RR.CC) proposed in
Xiao et al., (2023)
. In this article, we use our gradient decent algorithm with
d_{1}=d_{2}=0
to obtain the first-stage estimation. Then the solutions
\widehat{\mathbf{A}}_{1}^{\text{RR}}
and
\widehat{\mathbf{A}}_{2}^{\text{RR}}
are decomposed to obtain the initial value of
\mathbf{A}_{1}^{(0)}
and
\mathbf{A}_{2}^{(0)}
. Once we obtain minimizers in (
115
), we have an upper bound of initialization error.

Proposition 5
.

Under Assumptions
1
and
2
, when
T\gtrsim(p_{1}r_{1}+p_{2}r_{2}+4r_{1}^{2}r_{2}^{2})M_{2}^{-2}\max\left(\tau,\tau^{2}\right)
, with probability at least
1-C\exp\left(-C(p_{1}r_{1}+p_{2}r_{2})\right)
, we have

\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{A}}_{2}^{\mathrm{RR}}\otimes\widehat{\mathbf{A}}_{1}^{\mathrm{RR}}-\mathbf{A}_{2}^{*}\otimes\mathbf{A}_{1}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\lesssim\alpha_{\mathrm{RSC}}^{-1}\tau M_{1}\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{df}_{\mathrm{RRMAR}}}{T}},

and

\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{A}}_{1}^{\mathrm{RR}}-\mathbf{A}_{1}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{A}}_{2}^{\mathrm{RR}}-\mathbf{A}_{2}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\lesssim\phi^{-1}\alpha_{\mathrm{RSC}}^{-1}\tau M_{1}\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{df}_{\mathrm{RRMAR}}}{T}}.

Moreover, together with Assumption
3
, the initialization error satisfies

\mathrm{dist}^{2}_{(0)}\lesssim\phi^{2/3}\underline{\sigma}^{-4}g_{\min}^{-4}\alpha_{\mathrm{RSC}}^{-2}\tau^{2}M_{1}^{2}\frac{\mathrm{df}_{\mathrm{RRMAR}}}{T}.

Proof of Proposition
5
.

Let
\widehat{\mathbf{A}}^{\mathrm{RR}}=\widehat{\mathbf{A}}_{2}^{\mathrm{RR}}\otimes\widehat{\mathbf{A}}_{1}^{\mathrm{RR}}
. First, we give the error bound of
\widehat{\mathbf{A}}^{\mathrm{RR}}
. Let
\boldsymbol{\Delta}:=\widehat{\mathbf{A}}^{\mathrm{RR}}-\mathbf{A}^{*}=\widehat{\mathbf{A}}_{2}^{\mathrm{RR}}\otimes\widehat{\mathbf{A}}_{1}^{\mathrm{RR}}-\mathbf{A}_{2}^{*}\otimes\mathbf{A}_{1}^{*}
. By the optimality of
\widehat{\mathbf{A}}^{\mathrm{RR}}
,

\frac{1}{2T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left\|\mathbf{y}_{t}-\widehat{\mathbf{A}}^{\mathrm{RR}}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}\right\|_{2}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{2T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left\|\mathbf{y}_{t}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}\right\|_{2}^{2},

we have

\frac{1}{2T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}\right\|_{2}^{2}\leq\left\langle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{e}_{t}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top},\boldsymbol{\Delta}\right\rangle.

Define
a_{2}:=\mathrm{vec}(\widehat{\mathbf{A}}_{2}^{\mathrm{RR}})/\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{A}}_{2}^{\mathrm{RR}}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}},a_{1}:=\mathrm{vec}(\widehat{\mathbf{A}}_{1}^{\mathrm{RR}})\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{A}}_{2}^{\mathrm{RR}}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}},a_{2}^{*}:=\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{A}_{2}^{*}),a_{1}^{*}:=\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{A}_{2}^{*})
. By permutation operator,
\boldsymbol{\Delta}
can be decomposed as:

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Delta}=

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}^{-1}\mathcal{P}\left(\widehat{\mathbf{A}}_{2}^{\mathrm{RR}}\otimes\widehat{\mathbf{A}}_{1}^{\mathrm{RR}}-\mathbf{A}_{2}^{*}\otimes\mathbf{A}_{1}^{*}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}^{-1}\left(a_{2}a_{1}^{\top}-a_{2}^{*}a_{1}^{*\top}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}^{-1}\left(a_{2}(a_{1}^{\top}-a_{2}^{\top}a_{2}^{*}a_{1}^{*\top})+(a_{2}a_{2}^{\top}-\mathbf{I}_{p_{2}^{2}})a_{2}^{*}a_{1}^{*\top}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}^{-1}\left(a_{2}(a_{1}^{\top}-a_{2}^{\top}a_{2}^{*}a_{1}^{*\top})\right)+\mathcal{P}^{-1}\left((a_{2}a_{2}^{\top}-\mathbf{I}_{p_{2}^{2}})a_{2}^{*}a_{1}^{*\top}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbf{A}}_{2}^{\mathrm{RR}}\otimes\left(\widehat{\mathbf{A}}_{1}^{\mathrm{RR}}-\frac{\left\langle\widehat{\mathbf{A}}_{2}^{\mathrm{RR}},\mathbf{A}_{2}^{*}\right\rangle}{\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{A}}_{2}^{\mathrm{RR}}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}\mathbf{A}_{1}^{*}\right)+\left(\frac{\left\langle\widehat{\mathbf{A}}_{2}^{\mathrm{RR}},\mathbf{A}_{2}^{*}\right\rangle}{\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{A}}_{2}^{\mathrm{RR}}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}\widehat{\mathbf{A}}_{2}^{\mathrm{RR}}-\mathbf{A}_{2}^{*}\right)\otimes\mathbf{A}_{1}^{*}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbf{A}}_{2}^{\mathrm{RR}}\otimes\mathbf{W}_{1}+\mathbf{W}_{2}\otimes\mathbf{A}_{1}^{*}.

From definition we see that
\mathbf{W}_{1}
and
\mathbf{W}_{2}
are rank-
2r_{1}
and rank-
2r_{2}
matrices, respectively. Meanwhile,
\left\langle\widehat{\mathbf{A}}_{2}^{\mathrm{RR}}\otimes\mathbf{W}_{1},\mathbf{W}_{2}\otimes\mathbf{A}_{1}^{*}\right\rangle=0
. Hence, with Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,

\displaystyle\left\langle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{e}_{t}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top},\boldsymbol{\Delta}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{A}}_{2}^{\mathrm{RR}}\otimes\mathbf{W}_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\left\|\mathbf{W}_{2}\otimes\mathbf{A}_{1}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\right)

\displaystyle\times\left(\left\langle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{e}_{t}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top},\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}_{2}^{\mathrm{RR}}}{\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{A}}_{2}^{\mathrm{RR}}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}}\otimes\frac{\mathbf{W}_{1}}{\left\|\mathbf{W}_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}}\right\rangle+\left\langle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{e}_{t}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top},\frac{\mathbf{W}_{2}}{\left\|\mathbf{W}_{2}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}}\otimes\frac{\mathbf{A}_{1}^{*}}{\left\|\mathbf{A}_{1}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}}\right\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{A}}_{2}^{\mathrm{RR}}\otimes\mathbf{W}_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\left\|\mathbf{W}_{2}\otimes\mathbf{A}_{1}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\right)(\xi(2r_{1},r_{2},0,0)+\xi(r_{1},2r_{2},0,0))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}(\xi(2r_{1},r_{2},0,0)+\xi(r_{1},2r_{2},0,0)).

By Proposition
3
, when
T\gtrsim(p_{1}r_{1}+p_{2}r_{2}+4r_{1}^{2}r_{2}^{2})M_{2}^{-2}\max\left(\tau,\tau^{2}\right)
, we have that with probability as least
1-C\exp\left(-C(p_{1}r_{1}+p_{2}r_{2})\right)
,

\xi(2r_{1},r_{2},0,0)+\xi(r_{1},2r_{2},0,0)\lesssim\tau M_{1}\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{df}_{\mathrm{RRMAR}}}{T}}.

By Proposition
2
, with probability at least
1-C\exp\left(-C(p_{1}r_{1}+p_{2}r_{2})\right)
,

\frac{\alpha_{\mathrm{RSC}}}{2}\left\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{2T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}\right\|_{2}^{2}.

Combining the pieces together, we have when
T\gtrsim(p_{1}r_{1}+p_{2}r_{2}+4r_{1}^{2}r_{2}^{2})M_{2}^{-2}\max\left(\tau,\tau^{2}\right)
, with probability as least
1-C\exp\left(-C(p_{1}r_{1}+p_{2}r_{2})\right)
,

\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{A}}_{2}^{\mathrm{RR}}\otimes\widehat{\mathbf{A}}_{1}^{\mathrm{RR}}-\mathbf{A}_{2}^{*}\otimes\mathbf{A}_{1}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\lesssim\alpha_{\mathrm{RSC}}^{-1}\tau M_{1}\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{df}_{\mathrm{RRMAR}}}{T}}.

Since
\|\widehat{\mathbf{A}}_{1}^{\mathrm{RR}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}=\|\widehat{\mathbf{A}}_{2}^{\mathrm{RR}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}
, by (
87
),

\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{A}}_{1}^{\mathrm{RR}}-\mathbf{A}_{1}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{A}}_{2}^{\mathrm{RR}}-\mathbf{A}_{2}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\lesssim\phi^{-1}\alpha_{\mathrm{RSC}}^{-1}\tau M_{1}\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{df}_{\mathrm{RRMAR}}}{T}}.

Finally, with Assumption
3
, employing an argument analogous to Lemma B.4 in
Wang et al., (2023)
, we bound the initialization error as

\mathrm{dist}^{2}_{(0)}\lesssim\phi^{8/3}\underline{\sigma}^{-4}g_{\min}^{-4}\left(\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{A}}_{1}^{\mathrm{RR}}-\mathbf{A}_{1}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{A}}_{2}^{\mathrm{RR}}-\mathbf{A}_{2}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right)\lesssim\phi^{2/3}\underline{\sigma}^{-4}g_{\min}^{-4}\alpha_{\mathrm{RSC}}^{-2}\tau^{2}M_{1}^{2}\frac{\mathrm{df}_{\mathrm{RRMAR}}}{T}.

∎

Therefore, when
T
is large, the initialization condition for local convergence in Theorem
1
holds with high probability.

Proposition 6
.

Under Assumptions
1
,
2
and
3
, if
T\gtrsim g_{\min}^{-4}\kappa^{2}\underline{\sigma}^{-4}\alpha_{\mathrm{RSC}}^{-3}\beta_{\mathrm{RSS}}\tau^{2}M_{1}^{2}(p_{1}r_{1}+p_{2}r_{2})
and
T\gtrsim(p_{1}r_{1}+p_{2}r_{2}+4r_{1}^{2}r_{2}^{2})M_{2}^{-2}\max\left(\tau,\tau^{2}\right)
, with probability at least
1-C\exp\left(-C(p_{1}r_{1}+p_{2}r_{2})\right)
, we have
